ASSISTANT (final):
Great question — and the answer is unusually concrete here because the infrastructure to do exactly this already half-exists. Let me give you the grounded picture and a recommendation.

## What we already have

**The function list: yes.** `rust/sf-oracle/data/symbols.txt` has **13,623 labels** (name → SNES address) — every function, WRAM variable, and struct-field offset from the ultrastarfox build. The code-label subset (the `*_STRAT`, `*_ISTRAT`, `*_SROU`, `SR8_*`, `FIRE*`, etc.) is your ROM function inventory. So "the full list of functions from the ROM" is a solved input.

**The oracle can call any of them.** `sf-oracle` boots the real ROM and exposes `call(bus, addr, Entry{a,x,y,d,dbr,p}) -> Exit`, plus `read8/16`, `write8/16`, `wram_*`. It already has ~a dozen differential tests (mulslog, gen_3dvecs, do_coll, gsu_rotmat, speedto, audit_strats_b, audit_showview, audit_mapvm…). So per-function ROM-vs-Rust differencing is **proven**, not hypothetical — the mulslog fix this session came from exactly this (11,264 pairs, 3 latent bugs).

**What we don't have:** a machine-readable ROM→Rust mapping (it's implicit in the `ASM:file:line` citations in comments), and a systematic coverage ledger that says which of the 13k symbols is verified.

## The honest taxonomy (why "fuzz everything" isn't one button)

Your instinct is right, but functions split into two classes that need different harnesses:

**1. Pure / leaf functions** — math, trig, fixed-point, table lookups, distance/angle, RNG, the `SR8_*`/`s_*` helpers. Inputs = a few registers + memory cells; outputs = registers + touched memory. **Perfectly fuzzable, bit-exact.** This is the highest-ROI tier because the entire strat layer is built on these — fixing one latent (like mulslog) silently corrects everything downstream. Mostly automatable.

**2. Stateful strat/game functions** — `*_strat`, `fire_weapon`, `makeobj`, tick handlers. These read/write the *whole machine*: the alien array, hundreds of WRAM vars, the RNG stream, the object free-list, the sound queue, and they spawn children. You **can't** fuzz arbitrary inputs — 99% of random machine states are unreachable garbage that produce meaningless divergences. These need **whole-machine co-execution from *reachable* states.**

**On your side-effect categories specifically:**
- **Return value** → exit registers. Have it.
- **Memory modification** → snapshot WRAM + the object pool before/after and diff. Have it.
- **Audio** → *not* a waveform diff. `sf-spc` is already a bit-exact SPC engine port; the game-side reduces to **SE-trigger events** (which id fires when) — discrete and checkable (the sound-ID audit did a static pass of this).
- **Video** → *not* a framebuffer diff (different rasterizer, meaningless). The right invariant is the **draw list** — the geometry the game submits — which is already differentially audited (`showview`).

So "side effects" = memory + object pool + draw list + SE queue. All four are diffable snapshots.

## The plan I'd recommend

**A. Build the coverage ledger first** (the "1:1 mapping" you asked for). Auto-generate a table from `symbols.txt`: each code symbol → `{ported→rust_fn | pure-verified | unported | merged-into-X | data/N-A}`, cross-referenced against the ASM-label citations already in the Rust comments. *This is what makes 100% definable* — it converts "are we done?" into a checklist with a denominator.

**B. Tier-1: pure-function differential fuzz sweep.** Batch-expand the mulslog pattern across every pure leaf function — auto-harness, fuzz inputs, call ROM + call Rust, diff regs + touched memory bit-exact. Embarrassingly parallel (each function independent), cheap, and it drains the entire "latent math bug" class in one sweep.

**C. Tier-2: full-game co-execution trace diff.** Run the real ROM (sf-oracle already boots the game loop — that's how the RNG SWB chain was proven) and the Rust port side-by-side from the same seed/map with scripted inputs, diffing the *full* observable state every tick (all alien fields, gameplay WRAM, object free-list, SE queue, draw list). The **first per-tick divergence pinpoints the exact function and field**. Drive scripted inputs through every level/route/boss and it surfaces every remaining stateful divergence with a precise locator. This tier is what would actually *certify* behavioral 100% — matching every tick means it **is** the ROM. It's a generalization of the trace-parity fixtures I've been re-blessing, from hand-authored scenarios to continuous whole-game differencing.

The boss ports and wiring I'm doing now are, in effect, *expanding tier-2's reachable coverage* — you can't diff a boss fight the port can't reach.

**One decision that changes everything:** we currently diff against `load_built_rom()` (the ultrastarfox *source-built* ROM). There's also `load_retail_rom()`. The built ROM may itself differ slightly from the retail cart (the `base1` door caveat is exactly this — the hack's source ≠ original). "100% vs the built ROM" and "100% vs the retail ROM" are different targets. We should pick — or diff against both and treat retail as the tiebreaker.
----------------------------------------

### Turn 1285 @ 2026-07-08T03:28:30.301Z

ASSISTANT (final):
Ledger committed. Here's the honest strategic picture it produced — including a finding that reframes your "retail as truth" choice.

## The two things the ledger revealed

**1. Retail ≠ our symbol-mapped ROM (the important one).** The retail cart is 1 MB; the ultrastarfox ROM our 13,623 symbols address is 2 MB. They're genuinely different binaries — ~50% byte match, *every* bank differs. The built ROM is a source *reconstruction*, not a retail byte-repro (the `base1` caveat was the first crack in this). So:
- Per-function differential testing (tier-1) is inherently tied to the **built** ROM — that's the only thing with symbols. For pure math/logic it's equivalent to retail (reassembly doesn't change arithmetic — that's why the mulslog proof is trustworthy).
- **Retail-as-truth is best served by tier-2 whole-game co-execution**: boot the *retail* ROM, run scripted inputs, and diff *observable state* (object array, spawns, events, draw list, SE triggers) tick-for-tick against the port. This needs no symbol correspondence — only the retail object-array base + a few struct offsets (a small, bounded RE task). That's the path that actually certifies parity vs retail.

**2. The gap is bigger than "6 more bosses."** Beyond the 17/~22 bosses I've ported, the ledger surfaces a **long tail of unported regular enemies** never in my roadmap: bee, crab, saucer, tank, walker, wireman, shark, kami, evader, fastfighter, dragonfly, volrock, ripman, starbull… plus mini-bosses (amoeba, cruiser, core, crane, monolith) and the two XL capstones — **BOSSBROB (the Andross robot, ~80 sub-labels)** and **BOSSH**. The 43% name-match is a *floor*, not "43% done" (it undercounts — render/audio/math are covered structurally-differently, and many labels collapse many-to-one), but there's clearly substantial unported gameplay.

## My recommendation

Three tracks, and I'd run them in this order of leverage:

1. **Build tier-2 retail co-execution** — the certifier. It's the only thing that measures "100% vs retail," *and* it turns every subsequent enemy port from "hand-derived + hope" into "verified against the real cart." One-time cost: locate the retail object-array base + struct offsets.
2. **Tier-1 pure-function fuzz sweep** (built ROM) — cheap, parallel, drains the latent-math-bug class that silently corrupts everything downstream (like the 3 mulslog bugs). Do this in parallel; it's low-risk immediate wins.
3. **Port the long tail** — driven and verified by tier-2.

The single highest-leverage move is **tier-2**, because it's both the scoreboard and the safety net for all the remaining ports. But it has an RE dependency that could be fiddly, whereas tier-1 is a guaranteed quick win.

Which do you want me to build first?

=== achase_angle (enemy_a.rs ~303) ===
299:pub fn achase_angle(current: &mut u8, target: u8, shift: u32) -> bool {
pub fn achase_angle(current: &mut u8, target: u8, shift: u32) -> bool {
    if *current == target {
        return true;
    }
    let diff = (current.wrapping_sub(target) as i8) as i32;
    let mut step = if diff >= 0 {
        diff >> shift
    } else {
        -((-diff) >> shift)
    };
    if step == 0 {
        step = if diff > 0 { 1 } else { -1 };
    }
    *current = current.wrapping_sub(step as u8);
    false
}

/// C `add_player_z` (strat_enemy.c:55, s_add_playerZ): scroll with world.

    let al = &mut g.objs.aliens[idx as usize];
    al.worldz = al.worldz.wrapping_add(v);
}
=== strat_chase_proportional (common.rs ~315) ===
304:pub fn strat_chase_proportional(current: i16, target: i16, shift: u32) -> i16 {
pub fn strat_chase_proportional(current: i16, target: i16, shift: u32) -> i16 {
    if current == target {
        return current;
    }
    let diff = current.wrapping_sub(target);
    // adiv2^shift: arithmetic shift toward zero (not toward -inf). Compute the
    // negative branch in i32 like the C oracle's `int`: `-diff` overflows i16
    // when diff == i16::MIN (-32768) and panicked in debug; C widened to int.
    let mut step = if diff >= 0 {
        diff >> shift
    } else {
        -((-(diff as i32) >> shift) as i16)
    };
    if step == 0 {
        step = if diff > 0 { 1 } else { -1 };
    }
    current.wrapping_sub(step)
}

rust/sf-strat/tests/ea_units.rs:16:fn achase_angle_steps_match_rom() {
=== in rust/sf-strat/tests/ea_units.rs ===
fn achase_angle_steps_match_rom() {
    // ROM Achase (STRATMAC.INC:525 / SR8_ACHASE_ALVAR3, oracle-proven in
    // sf-oracle tests/audit_strats_b.rs): adiv2 rounds toward zero and the
    // reached-branch fires only when already at the target ON ENTRY.
    // diff = (int8)(0-128) = -128, step = -16 -> cur = 16.
    let mut cur = 0u8;
    assert!(!achase_angle(&mut cur, 128, 3));
    assert_eq!(cur, 16);
    // diff = (int8)(16-128) = -112, step = -14 -> cur = 30.
    assert!(!achase_angle(&mut cur, 128, 3));
    assert_eq!(cur, 30);
    // Toward-zero rounding: 0 -> 100 at rate 3 steps 100/8 = 12 (the old
    // floor shift gave 13; ROM gives 12).
    let mut cur = 0u8;
    assert!(!achase_angle(&mut cur, 100, 3));
    assert_eq!(cur, 12);
    // Small positive diff truncates to zero -> forced step of 1.
    let mut cur = 5u8;
    assert!(!achase_angle(&mut cur, 2, 3));
    assert_eq!(cur, 4);
    // Stepping onto the target still reports false; "reached" is an
    // entry-equality check, one tick later (ROM beq-before-step).
    let mut cur = 3u8;
    assert!(!achase_angle(&mut cur, 2, 3));
    assert_eq!(cur, 2);
    assert!(achase_angle(&mut cur, 2, 3)); // already there
    // Wrap-around chase picks the short way (8-bit signed diff).
    let mut cur = 250u8;
    achase_angle(&mut cur, 10, 3);
    assert!(cur > 250 || cur < 10, "cur={cur}");
}

// 1. sr8_achase_alvarN  (STRATROU.ASM:2763-2795)   ALL rates 1..7
//    ABI: entry A (8-bit) = target, mem[tpx] = current; result -> mem[tpx].
//    Rust equiv: sf_strat::enemy_a::achase_angle (8-bit, wraps mod 256).
// ===========================================================================
fn rom_achase8(rom: &[u8], addr: u32, current: u8, target: u8) -> u8 {
    let mut bus = SnesBus::new(rom.to_vec());
    bus.write8(TPX, current);
    call(&mut bus, addr, &Entry { a: target as u16, p: 0x20, ..Default::default() });
    bus.read8(TPX)
}

### Turn 1331 @ 2026-07-08T07:31:09.858Z

//! Byte-equality of the route1 (wave-2) level builders against the C oracle.
//!
//! Fixtures were dumped from the C MapBuilder (`src/map/levels.c`) by a
//! standalone harness that compiles levels.c with recording stubs for
//! `World_RegisterNativeCallback` / `World_RegisterInlineMapCode`, calls
//! `Levels_GetMapData(id)` once per map and writes `<name>.bin` (the blob)
//! plus `<name>.regs.txt` (`length`, `native 0x......` addr24s and
//! `inline <ptr>` script ptrs in C registration-call order) — the same
//! format as the phase-1 fixtures in `tests/fixture_parity.rs`.

use sf_map::catalog::{self, map_id};
use sf_map::levels::route1;

fn fixture(name: &str, ext: &str) -> Vec<u8> {
    let path = format!("{}/tests/fixtures/{name}.{ext}", env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"));
    std::fs::read(&path).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("read {path}: {e}"))
}

struct Regs {
    length: usize,
    native: Vec<u32>,
    inline: Vec<u16>,
}

fn parse_regs(name: &str) -> Regs {
    let text = String::from_utf8(fixture(name, "regs.txt")).unwrap();
    let mut regs = Regs { length: 0, native: Vec::new(), inline: Vec::new() };
    for line in text.lines() {
        let mut it = line.split_ascii_whitespace();
        match (it.next(), it.next()) {
            (Some("length"), Some(v)) => regs.length = v.parse().unwrap(),
            (Some("native"), Some(v)) => {
                let v = v.trim_start_matches("0x");
                regs.native.push(u32::from_str_radix(v, 16).unwrap());
            }
            (Some("inline"), Some(v)) => regs.inline.push(v.parse().unwrap()),
            _ => {}
        }
    }
    regs
}

    // Bless mode: the C harness that dumped these fixtures is gone (RIIR), and

    // macro emits count-1 — see MapBuilder::maploo

=== how mapobj encodes the strat param (builder.rs) ===
    pub fn mapnobj(&mut self, frame: i32, x: i32, y: i32, z: i32, shape: u16, strat: u32) {
        self.emit8(op::NORMOBJ);
        self.emit16(frame as u16);
        self.emit16s(x as i16);
        self.emit16s(y as i16);
        self.emit16s(z as i16);
        self.emit16(shape);
        self.emit16((strat & 0xFFFF) as u16);
        self.emit8(((strat >> 16) & 0xFF) as u8);
    }

    /// mb_mapobj: compact MAPOBJ when shape and strat both fit a byte,
    /// otherwise falls back to mapnobj.
    pub fn mapobj(&mut self, frame: i32, x: i32, y: i32, z: i32, shape: u16, strat: u32) {
        if shape <= 0xFF && strat <= 0xFF {
            self.emit8(op::MAPOBJ);
            self.emit16(frame as u16);
            self.emit16s(x as i16);
            self.emit16s(y as i16);
            self.emit16s(z as i16);
            self.emit8(shape as u8);
            self.emit8(strat as u8);
            return;
        }
        self.mapnobj(frame, x, y, z, shape, strat);
    }

    /// mb_maphardrot: mapobj with IS_HARDROT + sbyte1..3 rotation speeds + wait.
    pub fn maphardrot(&mut self, wait: i32, x: i32, y: i32, z: i32, shape: u16, rx: i32, ry: i32, rz: i32) {
        self.mapobj(0, x, y, z, shape, is::HARDROT);
        self.setalvarb(al::SBYTE1, rx);
        self.setalvarb(al::SBYTE2, ry);
        self.setalvarb(al::SBYTE3, rz);
        self.mapwait(wait);
    }

    // ---- flow / timing ----

    pub fn mapwait(&mut self, dist: i32) {
        self.emit8(op::WAIT);
        self.emit16(dist as u16);

/// C `clship_warp_cont` (strat_enemy.c:6496).
fn clship_warp_cont(g: &mut Game, idx: u16, zoff: i16, yoff: i16) {
    let pl = player(g);
    {
        let al = &mut g.objs.aliens[idx as usize];
        if al.sword1 > 0 {
            al.sword1 -= 1;
            if al.sword1 == 0 {
                // ASM clshipWARP_cont (GCSTRATS.ASM:143) jumps to clshipboost_Istrat
                // (:234 `trigse $32`) — the warp boost DOES play the sound. (Audit A #28)
                clshipboost_enter(g, idx, true);
                clshipboost_step(g, idx);
                return;
            }
        }
    }
    if let Some(pl) = pl {
        let al = &mut g.objs.aliens[idx as usize];
        // ASM GCSTRATS.ASM:148,152 `s_achase_alvar al_worldz/al_worldy` are the
        // PROPORTIONAL chase. (Audit A #8)
        al.worldz = chase_proportional(al.worldz, pl.worldz.wrapping_add(zoff), 4);
        al.worldy = chase_proportional(al.worldy, pl.worldy.wrapping_add(yoff), 4);
        let mut rotx = al.rotx;
        achase_angle(&mut rotx, pl.rotx, 5);
        al.rotx = rotx;
    }
    {
        let al = &mut g.objs.aliens[idx as usize];
        let mut rotz = al.rotz;
        achase_angle(&mut rotz, 0, 5);
        al.rotz = rotz;
    }
    add_player_z(g, idx);
    let w2 = g.vars.psvar_word2;
    let al = &mut g.objs.aliens[idx as usize];
    al.worldz = al.worldz.wrapping_add(w2);
}

### Turn 1395 @ 2026-07-08T10:25:23.033Z
USER:
//! End-to-end GSU validation: run the real `mcallarctan16` GSU program from the
//! ROM and check it computes atan2. This is what proves the GSU emulator is
//! trustworthy enough to validate GSU-side game functions (angle/3D math).
//!
//! ABI (from disassembling $01:81AA): inputs in GSU RAM (bank $70) m_x1=$62,
//! m_y1=$2C; result m_cnt=$40. arctan16 returns arctan(x1/y1), 0..359deg mapped
//! to $0000..$FFFF.

use sf_oracle::gsu::Gsu;
use sf_oracle::load_built_rom;

const M_X1: usize = 0x62;
const M_Y1: usize = 0x2C;
const M_CNT: usize = 0x40;

fn gsu_arctan(rom: &[u8], x: i16, y: i16) -> u16 {
    let mut gsu = Gsu::new(rom.to_vec());
    gsu.ram[M_X1] = x as u16 as u8;
    gsu.ram[M_X1 + 1] = (x as u16 >> 8) as u8;
    gsu.ram[M_Y1] = y as u16 as u8;
    gsu.ram[M_Y1 + 1] = (y as u16 >> 8) as u8;
    gsu.run(1, 0x81AA); // mcallarctan16 @ $01:81AA
    gsu.ram[M_CNT] as u16 | ((gsu.ram[M_CNT + 1] as u16) << 8)
}

fn expected_angle(x: i16, y: i16) -> u16 {
    let mut a = (x as f64).atan2(y as f64);
    if a < 0.0 {
        a += 2.0 * std::f64::consts::PI;
    }
    (a * 65536.0 / (2.0 * std::f64::consts::PI)).round() as u16
}

### Turn 1419 @ 2026-07-08T14:53:33.731Z
USER:
515:pub(crate) fn strat_find_near_shape(
558:pub(crate) fn strat_find_near_colltype(
pub(crate) fn strat_find_near_shape(
    g: &Game,
    self_idx: u16,
    shape_id: u16,
    exclude: Option<u16>,
    max_z: i16,
    max_xy: i16,
) -> Option<u16> {
    let me = g.objs.aliens[self_idx as usize];
    let mapped_shape = if shape_id < 256 {
        g.world.shapes_table[shape_id as usize]
    } else {
        shape_id
    };
    let mut best: Option<u16> = None;
    let mut best_metric: i32 = 0x7FFF_FFFF;
    for it in g.objs.active_indices() {
        if it == self_idx || Some(it) == exclude {
            continue;
        }
        let al = &g.objs.aliens[it as usize];
        if !al.active {
            continue;
        }
        if !(al.shape == shape_id || al.shape == mapped_shape) {
            continue;
        }
        let dz = (al.worldz as i32 - me.worldz as i32).abs() as i16;
        let dx = (al.worldx as i32 - me.worldx as i32).abs() as i16;
        let dy = (al.worldy as i32 - me.worldy as i32).abs() as i16;
        if dz > max_z || dx > max_xy || dy > max_xy {
            continue;
        }
        let metric = dx as i32 + dy as i32 + dz as i32;
        if metric < best_metric {
            best_metric = metric;
            best = Some(it);
        }
    }
    best
}

### Turn 1428 @ 2026-07-08T14:57:41.231Z
USER:
helper at 2974
fn port_find_near_shape(
    objs: &[(u16, i16, i16, i16)],
    self_pos: (i16, i16, i16),
    shape_id: u16,
    max_z: i16,
    max_xy: i16,
) -> Option<u32> {
    let (mx, my, mz) = self_pos;
    let mut best: Option<u32> = None;
    let mut best_metric: i32 = i32::MAX;
    for (i, &(sh, x, y, z)) in objs.iter().enumerate() {
        if sh != shape_id {
            continue;
        }
        let dz = (z as i32 - mz as i32).unsigned_abs() as i16;
        let dx = (x as i32 - mx as i32).unsigned_abs() as i16;
        let dy = (y as i32 - my as i32).unsigned_abs() as i16;
        if dz > max_z || dx > max_xy || dy > max_xy {
            continue;
        }
        let metric = dx as i32 + dy as i32 + dz as i32;
        if metric < best_metric {
            best_metric = metric;
            best = Some(i as u32 + 1);
        }
    }
    best
}

/// the port's `find_near_shape`.

    pub fn coldet_run(&mut self) {
        // Step 1: clear per-frame collision state (init_strats_ram_l
        // mirrors collide -> Lcollide before clearing collide).
        for k in 0..self.coldet.list.len() {
            let idx = self.coldet.list[k].alien;
            let al = &mut self.objs.aliens[idx as usize];
            if al.sflags & ASF_COLLIDE != 0 {
                al.sflags |= ASF_LCOLLIDE;
            } else {
                al.sflags &= !ASF_LCOLLIDE;
                // ROM init_strats_ram_l (COLDET.ASM:172-182): an object that is
                // NOT already colliding gets `al_collcount = 1` each frame

                // first do_coll on a fresh collision damage: do_coll DECs
                // collcount (1 -> 0) and the `BNE exit` falls through. The port

                // do_coll (DEC-then-BNE) a fresh collision saw collcount 0 -> 255

fn retail_same_shape_skip_divergence() {
    use sf_game::alien::{ACF_COLLTYPE2, ACF_COLLTYPE3, ASF_COLLIDE};
    use sf_game::game::Game;

    let mut g = Game::new();
    // Two objects, SAME shape, DIFFERENT colltype (enemy1 vs enemy2), overlapping.
    let a = g.objs.alloc().expect("alloc a");
    let b = g.objs.alloc().expect("alloc b");
    for (idx, ct) in [(a, ACF_COLLTYPE2), (b, ACF_COLLTYPE3)] {
        let al = &mut g.objs.aliens[idx as usize];
        al.shape = 0x0042; // SAME shape id
        al.collflags = ct; // clears ACF_FIRSTFRAME (alloc set it) so it enters the list
        al.hp = 50;
        al.ap = 5;
        al.sflags = 0;
        al.sflags3 = 0; // NEITHER has sameshapecollide -> cart would SKIP the pair
        al.worldx = 0;
        al.worldy = 0;
        al.worldz = 0;
        al.immuneptr = 0;
        al.sbyte4 = 0;
    }

    g.coldet_generate_list();
    g.coldet_run();

    let coll_a = g.objs.aliens[a as usize].sflags & ASF_COLLIDE != 0;
    let coll_b = g.objs.aliens[b as usize].sflags & ASF_COLLIDE != 0;
    eprintln!(
        "COLL same-shape: shape=$0042 both, colltype enemy1 vs enemy2, overlapping -> port collide=({coll_a},{coll_b})"
    );
    // RETAIL would NOT collide these (same shape, no sameshapecollide). The PORT
    // does — this pins the CURRENT (divergent) port behaviour. A follow-up
    // sf-game fix adds the same-shape gate and this expectation flips to false.
    assert!(
        coll_a && coll_b,
        "PORT currently collides same-shape different-colltype objects (divergence vs retail which skips). \
         If this fails, the same-shape gate was added to coldet_run — update this test to assert !collide."
    );
    eprintln!(
        "COLL: DIVERGENCE characterized — retail chkcoll0 SKIPS same-shape pairs (no sameshapecollide); \
         port coldet_run has no shape gate and collides them. Narrow blast radius (same shape + different colltype)."
    );
}

| System | Result vs cartridge |
|---|---|

Player-move — the last core shared system — came back **clean (no reachable bug)**. The three bugs all lived in enemy-A shared helpers and collision; the well-trodden core is now certified faithful. **The high-value shared-system bug vein is essentially mined out.** What remains:

- **Per-strat volume** (individual named strats) — the certifier confirms MATCH here; low new-bug yield.
- **Bosses** — genuinely hard to certify (child families, multi-phase state, GSU-per-tick), and a boss bug has narrow blast radius (one encounter).
- **Render/audio** — architecturally different by design.

## Honest bottom line

I've reached the point of genuine diminishing returns on autonomous certification. #!/usr/bin/env bash
# Star Fox HD — one-command launcher.
#
#   ./play.sh              # build (release) if needed, then play
#   ./play.sh --help       # forwarded to the binary
#   SF_PROFILE=debug ./play.sh
#
# Enters the nix dev shell (SDL3 / GL / Vulkan / Wayland / libdecor) and runs
# scripts/run.sh, which selects native Wayland + Vulkan when a Wayland session
# is present (falls back to the system default otherwise).
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")"
export SF_PROFILE="${SF_PROFILE:-release}"
exec nix develop --command ./scripts/run.sh "$@"

    pub fn begin_frame(&mut self, keys: &KeyboardState) {

        self.pad1 = Self::read_keyboard(keys) | self.read_gamepads() | self.autoplay_pad();

    fn resolve_path_start(&mut self, path_id: u16) -> u16 {
        // C Paths_ResolveStart (src/path/paths.c:2930) against the sf-path
        // literal catalog: valid offset -> offset; missing/out-of-range ->
        // warn once and return 0 (the remove-stub start).
        let catalog = sf_path::literals::get_catalog();
        if (path_id as usize) < catalog.offsets.len() {
            let off = catalog.offsets[path_id as usize];
            if off != 0xFFFF {
                return off;
            }
        }
        let mut st = self.state.borrow_mut();
        if (path_id as usize) < st.path_warned.len() && !st.path_warned[path_id as usize] {
            st.path_warned[path_id as usize] = true;
            println!("Paths: path id {path_id} is not ported yet; using remove-stub");
        }
        0
    }

    fn title_tick(&mut self) {
        if !self.title_loaded {
            // boot.c:143-149.
            self.game.objs = Objects::init();
            self.camera.init(&mut self.game.vars);
            self.game.world = World::init();
            self.reregister_strats();
            // TODO(wave3): sf_strat::table::register_all(&mut self.game)
            //   (C Strat_RegisterAll, boot.c:146).
            self.load_map(sf_map::catalog::map_id::TITLE);
            self.title_loaded = true;
        }

        // boot.c:152-155: clear list, Obj_RunStrategies directly (no
        // freezestrats guard, no coldet), camera, draw build.
        self.draw_list.clear();
        self.game.run_strategies();
        self.cam_snapshot = self.camera.update(&self.game.vars, &self.game.objs);
        // Cull anchors on the camera viewpos published by camera.update
        // (ROM viewposx/z, game.c:94-98), with the coldet shape-extents
        // table supplying the sh_zmax margin (alienflags_l MAIN.ASM:2030).
        draw::build_list(
            &mut self.game.objs,
            self.game.vars.playerflymode,
            self.game.vars.gameframe,
            self.camera.vars.viewposx,
            self.camera.vars.viewposz,
            self.cam_snapshot.ry as u8,
            self.game.vars.gameflags,
            &|shape| self.game.hooks.shape_extents(shape),
            &mut self.draw_list,
        );

        // Check for Start to enter gameplay (boot.c:158-164).
        if self.pad1_new & pad::START != 0 {

### Turn 1499 @ 2026-07-08T16:56:37.979Z
USER:
=== planets.update (nav input -> route) ===
    pub fn update(&mut self, press: u16) -> bool {
        if !self.route_converted {
            self.convertroute();
            self.route_converted = true;
        }

        let _ = self.drawplanetlines();

        if press & (pad::LEFT | pad::SELECT | pad::UP) != 0 {
            self.whichroute = normalize_route_choice(self.whichroute);
            self.whichroute = if self.whichroute == 0 { 2 } else { self.whichroute - 1 };
            let _ = self.drawplanetlines();
            return false;
        }

        if press & (pad::RIGHT | pad::DOWN) != 0 {
            self.whichroute = normalize_route_choice(self.whichroute);
            self.whichroute += 1;
            if self.whichroute >= PLANET_ROUTE_CHOICES {
                self.whichroute = 0;
            }
            let _ = self.drawplanetlines();
            return false;
        }

        if press & (pad::START | pad::A | pad::B) != 0 {
            self.stage = 0;
            self.currentplanet = 0;
            let _ = self.drawplanetlines();

            // planetseq_l converts route back before entering gameplay.
            self.convertroute();
            self.route_converted = false;
            return true;
        }

        false
    }
}

### Turn 1504 @ 2026-07-08T16:58:59.284Z
USER:
=== read_gamepads + axis/button handling ===
    fn read_gamepads(&self) -> u16 {
        let mut pad = 0u16;
        for gp in &self.gamepads {
            if gp.button(Button::South) {
                pad |= pad::B; // bottom = SNES B (brake)
            }
            if gp.button(Button::East) {
                pad |= pad::A; // right = SNES A (nova bomb)
            }
            if gp.button(Button::West) {
                pad |= pad::Y; // left = SNES Y (fire laser)
            }
            if gp.button(Button::North) {
                pad |= pad::X; // top = SNES X (boost)
            }
            if gp.button(Button::LeftShoulder) {
                pad |= pad::TLEFT;
            }
            if gp.button(Button::RightShoulder) {
                pad |= pad::TRIGHT;
            }
            if gp.button(Button::Start) {
                pad |= pad::START;
            }
            if gp.button(Button::Back) {
                pad |= pad::SELECT;
            }
            if gp.button(Button::DPadUp) {
                pad |= pad::UP;
            }
            if gp.button(Button::DPadDown) {
                pad |= pad::DOWN;
            }
            if gp.button(Button::DPadLeft) {
                pad |= pad::LEFT;
            }
            if gp.button(Button::DPadRight) {
                pad |= pad::RIGHT;
            }
            // Guide button as an extra Start source (some pads route the
            // menu/Start under Guide).
            if gp.button(Button::Guide) {

// ---- Frame API ----------------------------------------------------------

    pub fn set_clear_color(&mut self, r: f32, g: f32, b: f32, a: f32) {
        self.clear = [r as f64, g as f64, b as f64, a as f64];
    }

    /// Begin a frame: reset accumulation and acquire the surface texture.
    pub fn begin_frame(&mut self) {
        self.v3.clear();
        self.v2.clear();
        self.uniforms.clear();
        self.cmds.clear();
        if let Target::Surface { surface, .. } = &self.target {
            match surface.get_current_texture() {
                Ok(surface_tex) => {
                    self.pending_surface = Some(SurfaceFrame { surface_tex });
                }
                Err(_) => {
                    // Surface lost/outdated: reconfigure and skip this frame.
                    if let Target::Surface { surface, config } = &self.target {
                        surface.configure(&self.device, config);
                    }
                    self.pending_surface = None;
                }
            }
        }
    }

    fn push_uniform(&mut self, u: DrawUniform) -> u32 {
        let idx = self.uniforms.len() as u32;
        self.uniforms.push(u);
        idx
    }

    /// Draw solid-color 3D triangles (flat pipeline).
    pub fn push_flat_tris(

        proj: &[f32; 16],

        model: &[f32; 16],
        color: [f32; 4],
    ) {

            return;
        }

            proj: mat4(proj),
            view: mat4(view),
            model: mat4(model),
            color,
            mode: [0, 0, 0, 0],
            palette: [[0.0; 4]; 16],
        });
        let start = self.v3.len() as u32;
        self.v3.extend_from_slice(verts);

It's **intermittent** ("this time it went ok"), which rules out a deterministic logic bug — everything logic-side is verified clean (all routes enter gameplay headless, 400+ tests green). **Your binary now prints exactly why it exits.** Next time it closes on you, the terminal will show one of:
- `[exit] SDL Event::Quit received -> quitting` → a system/compositor/Steam quit event (likely **Steam Input** sending a close when Start is pressed, if the SC2 is running through Steam)
- `[input] controller N removed` → the SC2 briefly disconnecting on the button press
- `[exit] Escape key` → an unexpected keycode mapping

### Turn 1520 @ 2026-07-08T17:21:05.121Z
USER:
ohh i see. its because my sc2 was in desktop mode

ASSISTANT (final):
That explains it completely — mystery solved, and **it's not a game bug.** 🎯

In **desktop mode**, the Steam Controller 2 emulates keyboard/mouse instead of acting as a gamepad. So SDL sees it as "Controller connected: Steam Controller," but pressing what you think is "Start" actually sends a **desktop keyboard input** — almost certainly **Escape** (a very common desktop-config binding), which hits the game's `Event::KeyDown(Escape) → quit` handler. That's the clean `running = false` → "Shutdown after N ticks" exit, with no panic — exactly matching the symptom. And it was intermittent because it depended on the controller's mode.

**Fix on your side:** put the SC2 into **gamepad mode** (Steam's controller settings, or launch through Steam / the game's controller config) and it'll feed proper gamepad events — Start will advance the title instead of the desktop layer sending Escape.

The diagnostics did their job (they'd have printed `[exit] Escape key` to confirm) — and they're now committed, so they'll stay handy for the future.

**Optional hardening I can do if you want:** right now a single Escape instantly quits, which is what let the stray desktop-mode input kill the game. I could:
- Remove Escape-to-quit entirely (rely on the window close button / `Cmd/Alt+F4`), or
- Require **Escape-hold** or a confirm, so a stray Escape can't nuke a run.

Say the word and I'll wire one of those up. Otherwise, you're good to go once the SC2 is in gamepad mode.

=== port tit strat (enemy_b.rs 3957-4010) ===
/// ROM `tit_strat` (ENDSEQ.ASM:1805-1809):
/// `s_add_alvar B,x,al_rotz,#2` + `s_add_playerz x` — a constant-rate
/// Z-axis ROLL. (An earlier port spun al_roty instead, from a zero pose;
/// with the slot-0 player fallback the camera then yawed along with the
/// ship until the behind-cull freed it.)
fn strat_title_tick(g: &mut Game, idx: u16) {
    {
        let al = &mut g.objs.aliens[idx as usize];
        al.rotz = al.rotz.wrapping_add(2);
    }
    add_player_z(g, idx);
}

/// ROM `tit_istrat` (ENDSEQ.ASM:1799-1804): set the tilted display pose
/// (rotx = -deg45+deg11+deg11+3-deg5 = -17, roty = deg45+deg90 = 96,
/// rotz = 0; deg45=32/deg90=64/deg11=8/deg5=4, VARS.INC:13-17), then
/// `s_set_strat x,tit_strat` and fall through into the first tick.
pub fn strat_title_init(g: &mut Game, idx: u16) {
    let s = sid(g, strat_title_tick);
    {
        let al = &mut g.objs.aliens[idx as usize];
        al.hp = HARD_HP;
        al.ap = 0;
        al.collflags = 0;
        al.rotx = (-32i8 + 8 + 8 + 3 - 4) as u8; // 0xEF
        al.roty = 32 + 64;
        al.rotz = 0;
        al.stratptr = Some(s);
    }
    // ASM fall-through: tit_istrat runs straight into tit_strat.
    strat_title_tick(g, idx);
}
